### RHSA-2026:102813: Important: cockpit security update
- Source: Red Hat Security Advisories | Published: 2026-11-24T06:12:17Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: CVE-2026-76235, CVE-2026-91149 | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: An update for cockpit is now available for Red Hat Enterprise Linux 9.4 Update Services for SAP Solutions. Red Hat Product Security has rated this update as having a security impact of Important. A Common Vulnerability Scoring System (CVSS) base score, which gives a detailed severity rating, is available for each vulnerability from the CVE link(s) in the References section.
- Technical details: An update for cockpit is now available for Red Hat Enterprise Linux 9.4 Update Services for SAP Solutions.

Red Hat Product Security has rated this update as having a security impact of Important. A Common Vulnerability Scoring System (CVSS) base score, which gives a detailed severity rating, is available for each vulnerability from the CVE link(s) in the References section.

Cockpit enables users to administer GNU/Linux servers using a web browser. It offers network configuration, log inspection, diagnostic reports, SELinux troubleshooting, interactive command-line sessions, and more.

cockpit: Cockpit: Denial of Service via unbounded connection thread spawning (CVE-2026-91149)

cockpit-ws: cockpit: cockpit-ws: unauthenticated remote memory leak via CockpitLang cookie in send_login_html (CVE-2026-76235)

For more details about the security issue(s), including the impact, a CVSS score, acknowledgments, and other related information, refer to the CVE page(s) listed in the References section.

For details on how to apply this update, which includes the changes described in this advisory, refer to:

Red Hat Enterprise Linux Server for Power LE - Update Services for SAP Solutions 9.4 ppc64le

Red Hat Enterprise Linux for x86_64 - Update Services for SAP Solutions 9.4 x86_64

Red Hat Enterprise Linux for ARM 64 - 4 years of updates 9.4 aarch64

Red Hat Enterprise Linux for IBM z Systems - 4 years of updates 9.4 s390x

Red Hat Enterprise Linux for x86_64 - Extended Life Cycle 9.4 x86_64

Red Hat Enterprise Linux for ARM 64 - Extended Life Cycle 9.4 aarch64

Red Hat Enterprise Linux for Power, little endian - Extended Life Cycle 9.4 ppc64le

Red Hat Enterprise Linux for IBM z Systems - Extended Life Cycle 9.4 s390x

### OpenAI and Cisco Unveil AI-Assisted Linux Security Tool to Block Exploits Before Patching
- Source: The Hacker News | Published: 2026-11-24T06:02:57Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: ai_threat
- Summary: OpenAI and Cisco have teamed up to launch Shirudo (meaning "shield" in Japanese), an open-source Linux runtime that allows artificial intelligence (AI) agents to defend systems without downtime. "It lets agents write dynamic eBPF controls to shield systems from exploits, giving defenders more time to test and roll out patches," OpenAI said in a post on X. The AI upstart called the initiative a
- Technical details: OpenAI and Cisco launch Shirudo, an open-source Linux runtime that uses AI-generated eBPF shields to mitigate exploits without service downtime.

OpenAI and Cisco have teamed up to launch Shirudo (meaning "shield" in Japanese), an open-source Linux runtime that allows artificial intelligence (AI) agents to defend systems without downtime.

"It lets agents write dynamic eBPF controls to shield systems from exploits, giving defenders more time to test and roll out patches," OpenAI said in a post on X.

The AI upstart called the initiative a "first step" toward "self-defending infrastructure" that can adapt itself to new threats while the services continue to run and enable defenders to test and deploy fixes without any disruption.

The development comes as AI is accelerating vulnerability discovery, finding critical bugs in foundational software used to power internet-connected software.

Shirudo is being pitched as a way for defenders to test temporary safeguards (aka shields) and check if they block behavior that exposes a vulnerability, while letting legitimate requests pass through. Once a shield is approved by an operator, it can be deployed without having to stop the service.

The idea is to avoid a complete service disruption to block a line of attack simply because both an exploit and a legitimate request may rely on the same underlying Linux operation.

To achieve this, Shirudo makes use of eBPF (short for extended Berkeley Packet Filter), a technology that makes it possible to run lightweight, sandboxed programs inside the kernel without the need for modifying the system code or restarting it.

"eBPF is a highly efficient, sandboxed virtual machine in the Linux Kernel," Cisco's Isovalent said . "These eBPF programs allow you to interact with the Linux kernel to observe and take action on any events happening on a machine. Some use eBPF to simply observe everything on their system, others use it to enforce policies (routing, security, and more)."

Notably, Shirudo is built atop the foundations of Tetragon , an eBPF-based security platform that Isovalent developed and open-sourced in May 2022. The crucial difference is that it's primarily designed for agents and leverages the capabilities of eBPF to inject code mitigations.

Central to Shirudo is a guardian agent that uses eBPF shields to block vulnerable code paths without restarting the system, while giving security teams the flexibility to monitor how a proposed shield would affect the application before enforcing it. The shields are also cryptographically signed and verified prior to being loaded.

"Agents scan open-source code and take in vendor disclosures," Cisco said. "They trace each vulnerability to the affected assets and attack paths, then write a small, targeted shield. They check that the shield covers the exploit and that the service still runs. The shield only graduates to active blocking when the evidence says it's safe, and it retires when the real patch lands."

An agent is tasked with finding new vulnerabilities in an application, or working off external sources of new vulnerability disclosures.

Shirudo on the operator machine builds a security shield consisting of eBPF or application bytecode after reproducing and validating the exploit.

The shield is delivered as signed .bee packages via an OCI-compliant registry.

The Shirudo layer on each target pulls these .bee packages, validating each signature using TPMs to establish trust and safety.

In a controlled experiment, a Codex-generated shield blocked a Java4Shell exploit attempt against a running Elasticsearch service without requiring a restart, OpenAI said.

OpenAI and Cisco said the goal is to fight AI-based threats with AI and counter the risks posed by AI-driven attacks as soon as a vulnerability is identified at machine speed.

"We need this kind of machine-speed defense because AI models have become incredibly good at finding and exploiting software vulnerabilities, and they get better with every generation," Thomas Graf, CTO of Cisco Security, and Liz Rice, chief open source office at Isovalent, said. "They can also chain flaws together and discover creative new attack vectors that no one has thought about before."
- Fix / remediation: No specific fix identified in retrieved source text
- EPSS context: EPSS unavailable / not applicable — FIRST.org; not an asset-specific risk score

### Hackers Exploit N-able MailAssure Flaw While AI Agents Exploit MONAI Label Zero-Day
- Source: The Hacker News | Published: 2026-11-24T06:02:45Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: CVE-2026-133968, CVE-2026-134624 | Topic signals: active_exploitation, ai_threat
- Summary: The U.S. Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA) on Monday added two flaws impacting N-able MailAssure and MONAI Label to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities (KEV) catalog, citing evidence of active exploitation. The critical vulnerabilities are listed below - CVE-2026-134624 (CVSS score: 9.3) - A server-side request forgery (SSRF) vulnerability in N-able MailAssure's LDAP
- Technical details: CISA flags two exploited N-able MailAssure and MONAI Label flaws, including a zero-day AI agents used to gain root shell access.

The U.S. Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA) on Monday added two flaws impacting N-able MailAssure and MONAI Label to its Known Exploited Vulnerabilities ( KEV ) catalog, citing evidence of active exploitation.

CVE-2026-134624 (CVSS score: 9.3) - A server-side request forgery (SSRF) vulnerability in N-able MailAssure's LDAP synchronization connection check that could allow an authenticated API user to connect to arbitrary internal hosts and ports, determine which are reachable, and send crafted data to internal services.

CVE-2026-133968 (CVSS score: 9.8) - A path traversal vulnerability in MONAI Label that could allow an unauthenticated remote attacker to write arbitrary files outside the intended datastore directory, potentially resulting in remote code execution.

The exploitation of CVE-2026-134624 stems from a disclosure last week, in which N-able said it detected a security incident that resulted in a service disruption affecting Mail Assure and SpamExperts Hosted Cloud on November 16, 2026.

As of November 21, 2026, 11:44 p.m. UTC, access to the Mail Assure and SpamExperts Hosted Cloud dashboard and APIs has been restored.

"N-able has remediated the recent security incident affecting our Mail Assure and SpamExperts environments and restored the impacted functionality," a spokesperson for the company told The Hacker News. Our investigation to date indicates that exploitation was confined to a single N-able hosted server and continued monitoring since remediation has found no further malicious activity."

CVE-2026-133968, on the other hand, has been linked to an agentic attack in which a suspected China-nexus threat actor leveraged CAIRN , an artificial intelligence (AI)-powered problem-solving engine, to conduct hacking operations with minimal human involvement.

"In this campaign, the actor provided a list of targeted IP addresses with variations of the goal to 'obtain shell access,'" CJ Moses, CISO of Amazon Integrated Security, said. "Based on those inputs, the AI agents planned, executed, and adjusted their approach as they learned, without the operator choosing each next step."

Some of these attacks unsuccessfully attempted to brute-force their way into a Redis instance at one university and a GPU server at another. The activity also singled out an unspecified target running MONAI Label, an open-source medical imaging AI application, identifying the aforementioned zero-day and exploiting it in about 11 minutes to write arbitrary files to the server.

"In a later session, the agents used the same vulnerability to write a Python reverse shell payload to sitecustomize.py, a file Python automatically imports at interpreter startup," Moses said. "The agents then triggered a training API endpoint to spawn a new Python process. The new process imported the payload and established a reverse shell running as root."

Following the discovery of the zero-day, the operator is said to have provided the AI agents with a proxy to tunnel into a second university network running the same vulnerable software, and then directed them to target other machines in that network.

The attacks are part of a broader cluster of attacks involving the use of AI-driven frameworks like ARTEX , CAIRN , and AntiFox , and CyberStrikeAI in recent months. A subset of these activities is estimated to have breached over 100 companies, including John Deere and SharkNinja. The end goals, per NorForge , revolve around establishing persistent access and financial theft -

AIA0001, which has targeted organizations, mainly in the U.S., Japan, and Australia, to deploy web shells and gain access to e-commerce and payment infrastructure for subsequent monetization.

AIA0002, which has targeted merchant payment services, gray-market payment sites, pharmaceutical sites, and a criminal carding site for financial gain.

AIA0003, which has targeted smaller e-commerce sites and very large U.S.-based commercial platforms to obtain shell access, admin panel access, or database access.

In a report published last week, Amazon Web Services (AWS) also revealed that its MadPot sensor network captured requests from AI agents linked to two offensive campaigns running on ARTEX, an AI-native penetration framework.

"The most prominent ARTEX campaign was a large-scale theft of credentials to access AI models, organized as a self-sustaining operation in which stolen keys paid for the LLM inference powering further credential hunting," AWS said . "Another campaign targeted cryptocurrency exchanges for know-your-customer (KYC) verification bypass using real-time deepfake injection."

According to Datadog Security Labs, ARTEX also appears under other names like ScopeWeaver, LUCK — 自主资产控制台 (LUCK — Autonomous Asset Console), and 观澜SEC渗透测试平台 (Guanlan SEC Penetration Testing Platform).
- Fix / remediation: No specific fix identified in retrieved source text

For details on how to apply this update, which includes the changes described in this advisory, refer to:

The system must be rebooted for this update to take effect.

Red Hat recommends treating all kernel errata as security-relevant. Given the kernel's fundamental role, any bug has a higher chance of impacting system security, even if that impact only becomes clear after a fix is published. Therefore, Red Hat prioritizes delivering fixes that improve our customers' overall security posture.

Because of this proactive approach, a patch may be associated with a CVE assignment at a future date. Retroactive CVE assignments are always documented in the corresponding errata and on Red Hat's CVE pages. We strongly advise against delaying updates, as doing so may leave your system exposed when protections are already available.

Red Hat Product Security has rated this update as having a security impact of Important. A Common Vulnerability Scoring System (CVSS) base score, which gives a detailed severity rating, is available for each vulnerability from the CVE link(s) in the References section.

The buildah package provides a tool for facilitating building OCI container images. Among other things, buildah enables you to: Create a working container, either from scratch or using an image as a starting point; Create an image, either from a working container or using the instructions in a Dockerfile; Build both Docker and OCI images.

podman: Arbitrary file write when importing OCI archive (CVE-2025-11395)

crypto/x509: crypto/tls: golang: Go: Denial of Service vulnerability in certificate chain building (CVE-2026-32280)

net: golang: Go net package: Denial of Service via long CNAME response in LookupCNAME (CVE-2026-33811)

An update for kernel is now available for Red Hat Enterprise Linux 8.8 Update Services for SAP Solutions and Red Hat Enterprise Linux 8.8 Telecommunications Update Service.

Red Hat Product Security has rated this update as having a security impact of Important. A Common Vulnerability Scoring System (CVSS) base score, which gives a detailed severity rating, is available for each vulnerability from the CVE link(s) in the References section.

The kernel packages contain the Linux kernel, the core of any Linux operating system.

kernel: openvswitch: defer tunnel netdev_put to RCU release (CVE-2026-31678)

kernel: nvmet-tcp: check INIT_FAILED before nvmet_req_uninit in digest error path (CVE-2026-64534)

kernel: nvmet-rdma: handle inline data with a nonzero offset (CVE-2026-72129)

kernel: nvme: add missing SRCU grace period in error path (CVE-2026-89972)

kernel: nvme/ioctl: check SUBMIT_IO with nvme_cmd_allowed() (CVE-2026-90227)

kernel: tcp: prevent collapsing skbs across boundary in rtx queue (CVE-2026-98549)

kernel: kernel: Denial of Service via race condition in TCP socket lookup (CVE-2026-99288)

lpfc abort handler crashes after HBA reset (JIRA:RHEL-155283)

For more details about the security issue(s), including the impact, a CVSS score, acknowledgments, and other related information, refer to the CVE page(s) listed in the References section.

For details on how to apply this update, which includes the changes described in this advisory, refer to:

The system must be rebooted for this update to take effect.

Red Hat recommends treating all kernel errata as security-relevant. Given the kernel's fundamental role, any bug has a higher chance of impacting system security, even if that impact only becomes clear after a fix is published. Therefore, Red Hat prioritizes delivering fixes that improve our customers' overall security posture.

Because of this proactive approach, a patch may be associated with a CVE assignment at a future date. Retroactive CVE assignments are always documented in the corresponding errata and on Red Hat's CVE pages. We strongly advise against delaying updates, as doing so may leave your system exposed when protections are already available.

### USN-9189-1: Qt5Compat vulnerability
- Source: Ubuntu Security Notices | Published: 2026-11-23T23:21:59Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: It was discovered that Qt5Compat did not correctly handle certain text codec names, which could lead to an out-of-bounds read. An attacker could possibly use this issue to cause a denial of service.
- Technical details: Ubuntu is an open source software operating system that runs from the desktop, to the cloud, to all your internet connected things.

Thank you for contacting us. A member of our team will be in touch shortly. Close

Thank you for signing up for our newsletter! In these regular emails you will find the latest updates about Ubuntu and upcoming events where you can meet our team. Close

Your preferences have been successfully updated. Close notification

Qt5Compat could be made to crash if it received specially crafted input.

It was discovered that Qt5Compat did not correctly handle certain text codec names, which could lead to an out-of-bounds read. An attacker could possibly use this issue to cause a denial of service.

In general, a standard system update will make all the necessary changes.

The problem can be corrected by updating your system to the following package versions:

Ubuntu Pro provides ten-year security coverage to 25,000+ packages in Main and Universe repositories, and it is free for up to five machines.

### CVE-2026-134069 - Use of a default cryptographic key in the instance registration of Amazon SSM Agent on AWS Windows Server AMIs
- Source: AWS Security Bulletins | Published: 2026-11-23T21:54:18Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: CVE-2026-134069 | Topic signals: identity
- Summary: Bulletin ID: 2026-197-AWS Publication Date: 11/23/2026 13:30 PM PST Description: Amazon SSM Agent is the AWS Systems Manager agent that runs on EC2 instances so they can be managed through Systems Manager. We identified CVE-2026-134069, an issue in Amazon SSM Agent on AWS-provided Windows Server AMIs released before 2026-09-09, where instances launched from the same AMI shared one instance registration key. On instances managed through Default Host Management Configuration, a local user without administrative privileges might be able to obtain the instance's Default Host Management Configuration role credentials. Impacted versions: Amazon SSM Agent < 3.3.5226.0 on AWS Windows Server AMIs released before 2026-09-09. Instances launched from these AMIs remain affected after an in-place agent upgrade. Refer to the article for the most up-to-date and complete information.
- Technical details: Learn about CVE-2026-134069, a default key issue in Amazon SSM Agent on AWS Windows Server AMIs. Update now to ensure security. - AWS

Bulletin ID: 2026-197-AWS Scope: AWS Content Type: Important (requires attention) Publication Date: 11/23/2026 13:30 PM PST

Amazon SSM Agent is the AWS Systems Manager agent that runs on EC2 instances so they can be managed through Systems Manager.

We identified CVE-2026-134069 , an issue in Amazon SSM Agent on AWS-provided Windows Server AMIs released before 2026-09-09, where instances launched from the same AMI shared one instance registration key. On instances managed through Default Host Management Configuration, a local user without administrative privileges might be able to obtain the instance's Default Host Management Configuration role credentials.

Amazon SSM Agent < 3.3.5226.0 on AWS Windows Server AMIs released before 2026-09-09. Instances launched from these AMIs remain affected after an in-place agent upgrade.

This issue has been addressed in Amazon SSM Agent version 3.3.5226.0 , included in AWS Windows Server AMIs released on or after 2026-09-09. Instances launched from an affected AMI keep the shared key after an in-place agent upgrade, so we recommend replacing them with instances launched from these AMIs. Deleting the agent's Vault is not a supported remediation and breaks instances managed through Default Host Management Configuration.

If you cannot replace affected instances immediately, limit local, non-administrative user access on Amazon SSM Agent instances that are managed through Default Host Management Configuration and were launched from an affected AMI.

We would like to thank The YOSS Platform for collaborating on this issue through the coordinated vulnerability disclosure process.

Please email aws-security@amazon.com with any s ecurity questions or concerns.
- Fix / remediation: No specific fix identified in retrieved source text
- EPSS context: No EPSS score returned — FIRST.org; not an asset-specific risk score

### South Korea’s power giant KEPCO confirms data breach impacting 1.5 million
- Source: BleepingComputer | Published: 2026-11-23T20:54:51Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: apt_intrusion
- Summary: A threat actor breached the internal network of Korea Electric Power Corporation (KEPCO) and accessed two systems containing customer, employee, and contractor data. [...]
- Technical details: A threat actor breached the internal network of Korea Electric Power Corporation (KEPCO) and accessed two systems containing customer, employee, and contractor data.

The attack exposed details of more than 1.5 million individuals before the company blocked access from external IP addresses and suspended the affected services.

KEPCO is South Korea’s main electric power company and a state-controlled, major operator of the country’s critical infrastructure.

The organization detected the attack on November 20, when it confirmed unauthorized access to its KEPCO ON electricity billing platform and its electronic procurement system (SRM).

“On Friday, November 20, Korea Electric Power Corporation (hereafter KEPCO), led by President Kim Dong-cheol, confirmed that some customer information held in its IT systems had been exposed,” the power company states .

ON is an integrated customer platform that allows customers to view and pay electricity bills. SRM is the company’s procurement system for contracting services.

According to the announcement, published on Sunday, the following was exposed:

for 650,487 KEPCO ON customers: names, ID information, phone numbers, addresses, bank names, bank account numbers, account holder names, card issuers, card numbers, cardholder names, and electricity charge and billing information.

for 426,827 company representatives: names, ID information, email addresses, and company addresses.

for 442,934 KEPCO employees: names, affiliation, employee ID number, mobile phone number, email address, company phone number.

The company says the data breach resulted from “advanced technologies such as AI,” but it shared no specific details about the initial access method or the attack chain.

KEPCO is notifying exposed individuals directly, has filed a report with the data protection authorities, and is coordinating with government and financial authorities to block abuse such as unauthorized transactions.

Potentially exposed individuals are warned that the exposed information could be used for targeted phishing and impersonation. They are advised to report any attempts to the police or Korea’s Internet and Security Agency.

It is also recommended to avoid installing unknown apps, entering information into suspicious forms, and sharing sensitive data or verification codes over calls or texts.

A threat actor using the “Noiem” alias has claimed the attack and advertised the breach on a hacker forum on November 19, saying that they had KEPCO user data, supplier records, and bidding and contract documents.

South Korea’s Personal Information Protection Commission separately confirmed that KEPCO received an email from a hacker claiming data theft on November 19.

While these claims match the available evidence, attribution to Noiem remains unconfirmed.
- Fix / remediation: No specific fix identified in retrieved source text
- EPSS context: EPSS unavailable / not applicable — FIRST.org; not an asset-specific risk score

Cisco is investigating its product line to determine which products may be affected by this vulnerability. As the investigation progresses, Cisco will update this advisory with information about affected products.

If Cisco determines that a product is affected by this vulnerability, the first fixed releases will be listed in the following table. If a future release date is indicated, the date provided represents an estimate based on all information known to Cisco as of the Last Updated date at the top of the advisory. Available details indicate that systems that use x86-64 CPU architectures with AVX-512 VBMI support are affected. Hardware families that use x86-64 without AVX-512 or that use different CPU architectures like ARM or Cisco Silicon One are not affected.

There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.

For information about fixed software releases , consult the Cisco bugs identified in the Affected Products section of this advisory.

The Cisco Product Security Incident Response Team (PSIRT) validates only the affected and fixed release information that is documented in this advisory.

The Cisco PSIRT is aware that proof-of-concept exploit code is available for the vulnerability described in this advisory.

Customers of affected Oracle product(s) are strongly advised to apply the patches that were announced in these Security Alerts as well as those contained in this Critical Security Patch Update. Until you apply the Critical Security Patch Update patches, it may be possible to reduce the risk of successful attack by blocking network protocols required by an attack. ### CVE-2026-134056 - Out-of-bounds write in padded block cipher decryption in AWS-LC
- Source: AWS Security Bulletins | Published: 2026-11-23T20:29:41Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: CVE-2026-134056 | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: Bulletin ID: 2026-196-AWS Publication Date: 11/23/2026 12:00 PM PST Description: We identified CVE-2026-134056, an out-of-bounds write issue in AWS-LC's padded block cipher decryption. It affects applications that decrypt CBC or ECB ciphertext with PKCS7 padding over multiple update calls. If the output buffer is the minimum documented size, AWS-LC can write one byte past its end. This might crash the application or corrupt adjacent memory. This issue does not disclose data. The aws-lc-rs StreamingDecryptingKey API has a similar issue with cbc_pkcs7 and ecb_pkcs7. Its less_safe_update function can write up to one block (16 bytes for AES) past the buffer. Single-call decryption is not affected. Neither is decryption without padding, nor modes such as CTR, CFB, and GCM. Customers of AWS services do not need to take action. Refer to the article for the most up-to-date and complete informati
- Technical details: Bulletin ID: 2026-196-AWS Scope: AWS Content Type: Important (requires attention) Publication Date: 11/23/2026 12:00 PM PST

AWS-LC is an open-source, general-purpose cryptographic library.

We identified CVE-2026-134056, an out-of-bounds write issue in AWS-LC's padded block cipher decryption. It affects applications that decrypt CBC or ECB ciphertext with PKCS7 padding over multiple update calls. If the output buffer is the minimum documented size, AWS-LC can write one byte past its end. This might crash the application or corrupt adjacent memory. This issue does not disclose data. The aws-lc-rs StreamingDecryptingKey API has a similar issue with cbc_pkcs7 and ecb_pkcs7. Its less_safe_update function can write up to one block (16 bytes for AES) past the buffer.

Single-call decryption is not affected. Neither is decryption without padding, nor modes such as CTR, CFB, and GCM.

AWS-LC-FIPS 1.x is affected and reached end of support in October 2026.

This issue has been addressed in the following versions:

We recommend upgrading to the latest version and ensuring any forked or derivative code is patched to incorporate the new fixes.

Applications are not affected if they decrypt in a single update call. They are also not affected if the output buffer is at least the input length plus one block. Otherwise, there is no workaround. Upgrade to a fixed version. AWS-LC-FIPS 1.x reached end of support in October 2026 and will not receive a fix. Upgrade to a supported, fixed AWS-LC-FIPS release.

We would like to thank dywzju09 for collaborating on this issue through the coordinated vulnerability disclosure process.

Please email aws-security@amazon.com with any security questions or concerns.
- Fix / remediation: Upgrade to a supported, fixed AWS-LC-FIPS release. Applications using AWS-LC should upgrade to the most recent release of AWS-LC. Otherwise, there is no workaround and applications using AWS-LC should upgrade to the most recent releases of AWS-LC.
- EPSS context: No EPSS score returned — FIRST.org; not an asset-specific risk score

SQLite is a C library that implements an SQL database engine. A large subset of SQL92 is supported. A complete database is stored in a single disk file. The API is designed for convenience and ease of use. Applications that link against SQLite can enjoy the power and flexibility of an SQL database without the administrative hassles of supporting a separate database server.

### OpenRouter drops AI providers over leaked prompts and secrets
- Source: BleepingComputer | Published: 2026-11-23T20:00:20Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: ai_threat
- Summary: OpenRouter removed three AI providers from its platform after discovering that they had violated their stated data-handling policies and exposed sensitive data. [...]
- Technical details: OpenRouter removed three AI providers from its platform after discovering that they had violated their stated data-handling policies and exposed sensitive data.

The company also revoked Zero Data Retention (ZDR) eligibility for 125 endpoints as a precaution and notified all potentially affected customers.

OpenRouter is an AI model broker that lets developers access AI models from different companies using a single API key.

When an app sends a request, OpenRouter forwards it to a provider that runs the selected model and passes the answer back.

Operational technology security company Dragos alerted OpenRouter after sending two million test prompts through the platform, each containing a unique tracking link or decoy credential, and detecting "activity indicating that data from at least three AI providers was exposed."

Dragos discovered that, in some cases, those credentials were leaked to outsiders and used in the wild, indicating that the test prompts had escaped.

“We observed activity indicating that data from at least three AI providers was exposed. Our seeded test prompts were used in attempts to access our controlled test systems and canary AWS accounts,” Dragos says .

OpenRouter says there was no breach on its own systems, making the leak point at the provider level and leading to the decision to remove the three leaking inference providers: Together AI, Phala, and Novita.

Another provider, Atlas Cloud, said it would voluntarily withdraw its models from OpenRouter pending security testing, following a third-party security incident.

The other three published separate announcements responding to Dragos’ findings and explaining how the leaks occurred.

Together AI said an AI coding agent exposed a repository credential on GitHub, allowing an unauthorized party to download stored prompt samples from June and July involving MiniMax M3, GLM-5.2, and Kimi K2.6.

Phala said an attacker compromised a server processing DeepSeek V4 Flash 0731 requests routed through OpenRouter between October 21 and 22, and possibly up to November 8, and obtained application credentials from affected virtual machines.

Novita said a routing misconfiguration forwarded some MiniMax requests, including those sent through OpenRouter, to MiniMax’s official API between May 28 and November 11.

All four providers are tightening security controls, strengthening their zero data retention or data deletion policies, and advising potentially impacted users to rotate any secrets that may have been exposed in the leaked requests.

OpenRouter is also adapting Dragos’ tests for ongoing use to check whether AI providers forward or leak user requests/responses to third parties, and will require validation before restoring ZDR eligibility.

Adaptive Security's AI agents analyze email intent, context, and behavior to uncover phishing, business email compromise, and invoice fraud.

See what's getting through with a 14-day read-only scan of Microsoft 365 or Google Workspace, with no MX changes.

python-oauthlib - A generic, spec-compliant, thorough implementation of the OAuth request-signing logic

It was discovered that OAuthLib incorrectly handled JSONP callback parameters in the token revocation endpoint. A remote attacker could possibly use this issue to execute arbitrary code in a client application. ( CVE-2026-49264 )

Elvin Latifli discovered that OAuthLib incorrectly compared PKCE code verifiers, using a comparison that was not constant-time. A remote attacker could possibly use this issue to obtain access tokens, resulting in an account takeover. This issue only affected Ubuntu 26.10, Ubuntu 26.04 LTS, Ubuntu 24.04 LTS, Ubuntu 22.04 LTS, and Ubuntu 20.04 LTS. ( CVE-2026-49265 )

Strick Sheng discovered that OAuthLib incorrectly validated the client identity. A remote attacker could possibly use this issue to obtain tokens authorized under another client, bypassing token authorization. ( CVE-2026-118669 )

Kyle Hekhuis discovered that OAuthLib incorrectly validated device codes in the device authorization workflow. A remote attacker could possibly use this issue to obtain a token without completing device authorization. This issue only affected Ubuntu 26.10 and Ubuntu 26.04 LTS. ( CVE-2026-49272 )

In general, a standard system update will make all the necessary changes.

### Microsoft Azure flaw exploited to steal customers' API credentials
- Source: BleepingComputer | Published: 2026-11-23T19:42:20Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: identity
- Summary: Microsoft is warning customers that a security vulnerability in Azure API Management (APIM) Classic tiers was exploited to steal stored API credentials, with Norwegian authorities linking the flaw to cyberattacks against energy companies. [...]
- Technical details: Microsoft is warning customers that a security vulnerability in Azure API Management (APIM) Classic tiers was exploited to steal stored API credentials, with Norwegian authorities linking the flaw to cyberattacks against energy companies.

Azure API Management is a cloud service that lets organizations publish, secure, monitor, and manage APIs that connect applications, services, and data.

In an Azure Service Health security advisory (Tracking ID: MZK9-GH0) sent to customers and shared with BleepingComputer, Microsoft said it identified the vulnerability on November 18, 2026, and deployed a mitigation the following day.

"Our investigation found that customers were exposed and an unauthorized party could have used the issue to access secrets stored in Azure API Management Classic instances before Microsoft identified the security issue," Microsoft said in the advisory.

Microsoft told BleepingComputer that the vulnerability impacted the Developer, Basic, Standard, and Premium tiers of Azure API Management (APIM) Classic, but that the Consumption tier of APIM Classic and all APIM v2 tiers were not affected.

According to Microsoft, the vulnerability allowed unauthorized access to certain management functionality when APIM Classic instances were not configured according to recommended security best practices.

This access could have allowed attackers to perform administrative operations, including accessing secrets stored in the service.

Microsoft said the following information may have been exposed:

Client secrets associated with OpenID Connect providers

These credentials could potentially allow attackers to access additional applications, services, or backend systems, depending on their permissions.

Microsoft said it closed the unauthorized access path on November 19 and rotated Microsoft-controlled keys and credentials where appropriate.

However, the company warned that its remediation would not invalidate customer credentials that may have been compromised before the fix was deployed.

"This remediation blocks the unauthorized management-plane access path that could allow an actor to perform administrative operations, including secrets management, against Azure API Management services," Microsoft warned.

"However, it does not automatically invalidate credentials that may have been accessed before the fix was deployed."

The vulnerability affects Azure API Management Classic tiers, while Microsoft said APIM v2 tiers are not impacted.

Microsoft urged affected customers to immediately rotate potentially exposed credentials, investigate suspicious activity in systems accessible through those credentials, and review available logs for signs of unauthorized access.

In a Reddit post , an administrator claimed their organization discovered the vulnerability after detecting unauthorized access to Azure Key Vault using a valid administrator token.

### Spanish LaLiga confirms hackers stole soccer fans’ personal data
- Source: BleepingComputer | Published: 2026-11-23T18:38:40Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: LaLiga, Spain's top men's football league, disclosed a data breach that exposed personal information belonging to users of its services and those of some affiliated clubs. [...]
- Technical details: LaLiga, Spain's top men's football league, disclosed a data breach that exposed personal information belonging to users of its services and those of some affiliated clubs.

LaLiga, Spain's top men's professional football league, disclosed a data breach that exposed personal information belonging to users of its services and those of some affiliated clubs.

According to the organization, the incident occurred between November 14 and 16, and the attacker extracted data from compromised systems.

LaLiga organizes Spain’s top two men’s professional football divisions.

The top league is one of the world’s most prominent football leagues, where clubs with a global audience, such as Real Madrid, FC Barcelona, and Atlético Madrid, play.

On November 16, the organization’s security team identified the source of the unauthorized access and implemented measures to block the attack and stop the data extraction.

“LALIGA was the target of a cyberattack on one of its technology platforms for fan services,” reads the announcement .

“Between November 14 and 16, a cybercriminal gained unauthorized access to the platform and extracted personal data belonging to users registered for digital services offered by LALIGA and some clubs.”

LaLiga underlined that the incident was confined to a specific platform and did not extend to other systems that support the running of the competitions.

After implementing containment measures, LaLiga launched an investigation to determine the scope of the incident and introduced more thorough monitoring to detect future attempts to breach its systems.

The football organizer also reported the incident to the Spanish Data Protection Agency (AEPD) and filed a complaint with the National Police’s Central Cybercrime Unit.

Impacted clubs were also directly notified, and actions to inform impacted fans are being coordinated.

At the time of writing, no ransomware group has claimed responsibility for the attack on LaLiga.

BleepingComputer has contacted the organization to learn more about the attack and which clubs were impacted, but we have not received a response as of publication.

### Webworm Suspected of Modifying SoftEther VPN to Evade Detection and Hide Remote Access
- Source: The Hacker News | Published: 2026-11-23T17:45:15Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: apt_intrusion, edge_vpn
- Summary: The China-aligned threat actor known as Webworm is suspected to be using a custom version of the open-source SoftEther VPN Bridge tool in an attempt to remove recognizable detection fingerprints, while disguising it as network-monitoring software to establish covert remote access to compromised networks. The modified variant "implements various tricks to hide itself from security tools,"
- Technical details: Rapid7 links a stealth-focused SoftEther VPN Bridge variant to Webworm with moderate confidence, detailing changes designed to evade detection.

The China-aligned threat actor known as Webworm is suspected to be using a custom version of the open-source SoftEther VPN Bridge tool in an attempt to remove recognizable detection fingerprints, while disguising it as network-monitoring software to establish covert remote access to compromised networks.

The modified variant "implements various tricks to hide itself from security tools," Rapid7 said in an analysis, attributing it to the adversarial collective with moderate confidence.

First publicly detailed by Broadcom-owned Symantec in September 2022, Webworm is the name assigned to a China-aligned nation-state group that has targeted government entities, IT services, and electric power companies across Russia, Georgia, Mongolia, and several other Asian nations.

One of the tools used by the threat actor is a legitimate software called SoftEther VPN , with the bridge acting as a pathway connecting the compromised network and the attacker-controlled infrastructure.

The latest findings from Rapid7 revolve around a Windows executable (" netcapa.exe "), which is a modified build of SoftEther VPN Bridge ("vpnbridge_x64.exe") version 4.44, build 9807. The mutated version, rebranded as NtMonitor VPN, masquerades itself as Network Monitor Software from a company called Network Systems Solutions Inc. The artifact was uploaded to VirusTotal around mid-January 2026.

"The PDB path included in the executable reads C:\Users\Administrator\Desktop\SoftEtherVPN_Stable\SoftEtherVPN_Stable\SoftEtherVPN_Stable\src\DebugFiles\pdb\x64_Release\vpnbridge.pdb," the cybersecurity company said. "As such, the tool doesn't hide its identity from reverse engineers, only external interactions."

The renaming is also said to be inconsistent, with many registry key paths still referencing the program as "SoftEther Project," even though a number of other occurrences of "SoftEther VPN" have been replaced by "NtMonitor VPN."

One of the most noticeable changes identified in the altered version is the use of innocuous-looking HTTP endpoints "/update/alive.cgi" and "/update/picture.cgi" to replace "/vpnsvc/vpn.cgi" and "/vpnsvc/connect.cgi," respectively, so that they look like ordinary web application activity.

It also changes the protocol watermark used by SoftEther VPN as part of the handshake process when initiating communication to help the tool distinguish protocol traffic from ordinary HTTP requests. When the connecting client is found to speak the expected protocol, it proceeds with the handshake. If the check fails, the server returns a decoy HTTP response.

The modified version swaps the GIF-based watermark used by the stock SoftEther VPN utility for a PNG-based watermark so as to bypass existing detection signatures based on the GIF watermark.

Some of the other changes introduced to the program are listed below -

Reducing a resource archive named hamcore.se2 used by SoftEther VPN to its bare minimum, as it's required to be present at runtime, and embedding it directly into the executable, causing the binary's size to increase by about 1.5 MB.

Changing the name of the configuration file from "vpn_bridge.config" to "setting.conf" and making its contents encrypted with a custom algorithm. The encrypted configuration starts with the magic bytes "RCEN" in ASCII.

What's interesting here is that the attackers have not been found to introduce any new functionality to the tool, indicating the primary focus was on stealth.

"It seems like they methodically analyzed SoftEther's detection surface and implemented counter-measures to all of the easy ways a detection engineer may target it," Rapid7 said, calling the latest evolution a hybrid approach that brings together the attacker's use of custom malware and dual-use tools.

"We are observing a sort of hybrid of the two — stock SoftEther software that is not trojanized, but rebuilt from source in a way that erases much of its detection fingerprint."
- Fix / remediation: No specific fix identified in retrieved source text

### USN-9187-1: libssh vulnerabilities
- Source: Ubuntu Security Notices | Published: 2026-11-23T17:32:05Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: CVE-2026-59843, CVE-2026-59846, CVE-2026-59847, CVE-2026-59848, CVE-2026-59850 | Topic signals: identity
- Summary: Haruto Kimura, Rinku Das, and Yi Lin discovered that libssh did not correctly handle SSH channel open messages advertising a zero maximum packet size. An authenticated user could possibly use this issue to cause libssh to use excessive resources, leading to a denial of service. This issue only affected Ubuntu 16.04 LTS, Ubuntu 18.04 LTS, and Ubuntu 20.04 LTS. (CVE-2026-59843) Mikhail Ilin and Saransh Rana discovered that libssh did not correctly sanitize shell metacharacters when expanding usernames in ProxyCommand strings. An attacker could possibly use this issue to obtain sensitive information. (CVE-2026-59846) Ben Smyth discovered that libssh incorrectly verified AES-GCM authentication tags when built with the OpenSSL backend. A remote attacker in a machine-in-the-middle position could possibly use this issue to modify encrypted traffic without detection. This issue only affected Ubu

Haruto Kimura, Rinku Das, and Yi Lin discovered that libssh did not correctly handle SSH channel open messages advertising a zero maximum packet size. An authenticated user could possibly use this issue to cause libssh to use excessive resources, leading to a denial of service. This issue only affected Ubuntu 16.04 LTS, Ubuntu 18.04 LTS, and Ubuntu 20.04 LTS. ( CVE-2026-59843 )

Mikhail Ilin and Saransh Rana discovered that libssh did not correctly sanitize shell metacharacters when expanding usernames in ProxyCommand strings. An attacker could possibly use this issue to obtain sensitive information. ( CVE-2026-59846 )

Ben Smyth discovered that libssh incorrectly verified AES-GCM authentication tags when built with the OpenSSL backend. A remote attacker in a machine-in-the-middle position could possibly use this issue to modify encrypted traffic...

Ben Smyth discovered that libssh incorrectly verified AES-GCM authentication tags when built with the OpenSSL backend. A remote attacker in a machine-in-the-middle position could possibly use this issue to modify encrypted traffic without detection. This issue only affected Ubuntu 20.04 LTS. ( CVE-2026-59847 )

It was discovered that libssh did not correctly handle SFTP server responses for unknown request IDs. A remote attacker controlling a malicious SFTP server could possibly use this issue to cause libssh to use excessive resources, leading to a denial of service. ( CVE-2026-59848 )

Zhou Qingyang discovered that libssh could invoke data callbacks on channels after they had been closed, leading to a use-after-free. A remote attacker could possibly use this issue to cause libssh to crash, resulting in a denial of service, or possibly execute arbitrary code. This issue only affected Ubuntu 16.04 LTS, Ubuntu 18.04 LTS, and Ubuntu 20.04 LTS. ( CVE-2026-59850 )

In general, a standard system update will make all the necessary changes.

The problem can be corrected by updating your system to the following package versions:

Ubuntu Pro provides ten-year security coverage to 25,000+ packages in Main and Universe repositories, and it is free for up to five machines.

### GlobeTopper loses $2.5 million after hackers steal $70k in gift cards
- Source: BleepingComputer | Published: 2026-11-23T16:50:01Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: A cyberattack disrupted operations at B2B top-up solutions provider GlobeTopper, causing an estimated $2.5 million in lost revenue, while the attacker redeemed digital gift cards worth $70,000. [...]
- Technical details: A cyberattack disrupted operations at B2B top-up solutions provider GlobeTopper, causing an estimated $2.5 million in lost revenue, while the attacker redeemed digital gift cards worth $70,000.

Parent company iQSTEL said that the intrusion was detected on October 22 and GlobeTopper responded by suspending its platform and starting an investigation.

GlobeTopper is a business-to-business digital gift card distributor. Various retailers, fintech apps, and rewards platforms use its API. According to the platform’s website , it is used by more than 4,000 brands across 65 countries, including IKEA, Adidas, Spotify, Target, and Roblox.

In a filing with the U.S. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), iQSTEL said that GlobeTopper detected more unauthorized activity the day after it identified the attack. In response, the company "took the platform and all affected servers offline" in the early hours of October 23 to investigate and fix the problem.

The company deployed a completely rebuilt platform on AWS on October 31, while commercial operations were restored in stages, starting November 1.

GlobeTopper says the attacker obtained certain client API credentials and integration keys, which have been revoked and replaced, and it has observed no unauthorized use of them yet.

“During its investigation, the Company determined that the unauthorized party obtained API credentials and integration keys of certain GlobeTopper business customers,” iQSTEL says .

“All affected credentials and integration keys have been revoked and rotated, and affected business customers have been issued new credentials subject to additional access restrictions.”

The company further stated that an investigation is ongoing to determine whether other customer, personal, payment, or provider data was accessed or acquired.

Regarding the digital gift cards the attackers redeemed, the company says it cannot block, recover, or reclaim them, and will incur the losses.

iQSTEL said the incident hurt GlobeTopper’s fourth-quarter transaction volume and operating results, but as of the filing, it has not determined it to be material.

BleepingComputer has emailed iQSTEL with additional questions regarding the incident, but the company declined to share further information.

At the time of writing, no ransomware groups have listed iQSTEL or GlobeTopper as victims on their extortion portals.

### Cisco Expressway Series Remote Code Execution Vulnerability
- Source: Cisco PSIRT | Published: 2026-11-23T15:51:28Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: CVE-2026-76466 | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: A vulnerability in the database service feature of Cisco Expressway Series could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker with network access to a Cisco Expressway device to execute arbitrary operating system commands as root . This vulnerability is due to the use of persistent cookies. Additionally, there is insufficient validation of client certificates when the TLS Verify Mode is set to Enabled for clustering, which means that client certificates are not validated. An attacker with possession of the cookies could exploit this vulnerability by connecting to the database service and invoking specific remote procedure call (RPC) commands. A successful exploit could allow the attacker to execute arbitrary commands as root on the underlying operating system of an affected device. Note: Cisco Expressway Series refers to Cisco Expressway Core (Expressway-C) devices and Cisco Expressway Edge
- Technical details: A vulnerability in the database service feature of Cisco Expressway Series could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker with network access to a Cisco Expressway device to execute arbitrary operating system commands as root. This vulnerability is due to the use of persistent cookies. Additionally, there is insufficient validation of client certificates when the TLS Verify Mode is set to Enabled for clustering, which means that client certificates are not validated. An attacker with possession of the cookies could exploit this vulnerability by connecting to the database service and invoking specific remote procedure call (RPC) commands. A successful exploit could allow the attacker to execute arbitrary commands as root on the underlying operating system of an affected device. Note: Cisco Expressway Series refers to Cisco Expressway Core (Expressway-C) devices and Cisco Expressway Edge (Expressway-E) devices. Cisco has released software updates that address this vulnerability. There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability. This advisory is available at the following link:https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/content/CiscoSecurityAdvisory/cisco-sa-vcs-static-cred-hr7EWZ8t This advisory is part of a group of advisories. For a complete list of the advisories and links to them, see Cisco Advance Notification for Publication of October 21, 2026, Security Advisories. In addition, for further documentation of improvements and fixes in Cisco Expressway Series see Cisco Expressway Series Security Hardening Release: October 2026.

A vulnerability in the database service feature of Cisco Expressway Series could allow an unauthenticated, remote attacker with network access to a Cisco Expressway device to execute arbitrary operating system commands as root .

This vulnerability is due to the use of persistent cookies. Additionally, there is insufficient validation of client certificates when the TLS Verify Mode is set to Enabled for clustering, which means that client certificates are not validated. An attacker with possession of the cookies could exploit this vulnerability by connecting to the database service and invoking specific remote procedure call (RPC) commands. A successful exploit could allow the attacker to execute arbitrary commands as root on the underlying operating system of an affected device.

Note: Cisco Expressway Series refers to Cisco Expressway Core (Expressway-C) devices and Cisco Expressway Edge (Expressway-E) devices.

Cisco has released software updates that address this vulnerability. There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.

This advisory is available at the following link: https://sec.cloudapps.cisco.com/security/center/content/CiscoSecurityAdvisory/cisco-sa-vcs-static-cred-hr7EWZ8t

This advisory is part of a group of advisories. For a complete list of the advisories and links to them, see Cisco Advance Notification for Publication of October 21, 2026, Security Advisories . In addition, for further documentation of improvements and fixes in Cisco Expressway Series see Cisco Expressway Series Security Hardening Release: October 2026 .

This vulnerability affects Cisco Expressway-C and Cisco Expressway-E, both virtual and appliance versions, if they are running a vulnerable release and have the devices configured in a cluster.

For information about which Cisco software releases are vulnerable, see the Fixed Software section of this advisory.

Only products listed in the Vulnerable Products section of this advisory are known to be affected by this vulnerability.

There are no workarounds that address this vulnerability.

Cisco considers any workarounds and mitigations (if applicable) to be temporary solutions until an upgrade to a fixed software release is available. To remediate this vulnerability and avoid future exposure as described in this advisory, Cisco strongly recommends that customers upgrade to the fixed software indicated in this advisory.

In the following table, the left column lists Cisco software releases. The right column indicates whether a release is affected by the vulnerability that is described in this advisory and the first release that includes the fix for this vulnerability. Customers are advised to upgrade to an appropriate fixed software release as indicated in this section.

Note: This fix is also included as part of the broader hardening improvements that are disclosed in the security advisory Cisco Expressway Series Security Hardening Release: October 2026 .

The Cisco Product Security Incident Response Team (PSIRT) validates only the affected and fixed release information that is documented in this advisory.

In November 2026, this vulnerability was added to the Known Exploited Vulnerability (KEV) database of the U.S. Cybersecurity and Infrastructure Security Agency (CISA). Cisco continues to strongly recommend that customers upgrade to a fixed software release to remediate this vulnerability.

Cisco would like to thank Stefan Aleksic of Jane Street for reporting this vulnerability.

To learn about Cisco security vulnerability disclosure policies and publications, see the Security Vulnerability Policy . This document also contains instructions for obtaining fixed software and receiving security vulnerability information from Cisco.

For additional information on Cisco's vulnerability management, disclosure cadence, and software patching strategy, see Cisco's Transition to a Risk-Based Vulnerability Disclosure Model .
- Fix / remediation: No specific fix identified in retrieved source text

### ⚡ Weekly Recap: VMware 0-Day, Java4Shell, NGINX2Root, Ruby2Shell, and a Whole Lot of Shell Trouble
- Source: The Hacker News | Published: 2026-11-23T15:10:48Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: CVE-2026-112115, CVE-2026-113425, CVE-2026-114202, CVE-2026-114204, CVE-2026-114205, CVE-2026-114819, CVE-2026-114938, CVE-2026-116953, CVE-2026-119618, CVE-2026-120285, CVE-2026-120292, CVE-2026-120718, CVE-2026-120722, CVE-2026-121805, CVE-2026-121810, CVE-2026-121812, CVE-2026-123007, CVE-2026-123633, CVE-2026-123634, CVE-2026-123637, CVE-2026-123890, CVE-2026-125272, CVE-2026-125274, CVE-2026-125396, CVE-2026-126336, CVE-2026-126574, CVE-2026-126822, CVE-2026-129975, CVE-2026-130963, CVE-2026-130975, CVE-2026-131008, CVE-2026-131031, CVE-2026-131054, CVE-2026-131060, CVE-2026-131914, CVE-2026-132165, CVE-2026-132798, CVE-2026-132825, CVE-2026-134624, CVE-2026-134814, CVE-2026-14978, CVE-2026-23871, CVE-2026-28400, CVE-2026-45110, CVE-2026-49401, CVE-2026-59890, CVE-2026-99312, CVE-2026-99362 | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: A stolen key. A trusted message. A routine update. None of these looks like the start of a major security problem. That's partly why they work. Attackers don't always need to break down a door. Sometimes they just use one that's already open. This week's stories show how quickly a small opening can turn into a much bigger mess. - Technical details: This week's cyber roundup covers VMware 0-day attacks, Java4Shell, NGINX2Root, Ruby2Shell, AI supply chain threats, router backdoors, and more.

A stolen key. A trusted message. A routine update. None of these looks like the start of a major security problem. That's partly why they work. Attackers don't always need to break down a door. Sometimes they just use one that's already open.

This week's stories show how quickly a small opening can turn into a much bigger mess. One breach helps attackers reach another target. Tools people rely on become ways to get inside. Even features built for safety can end up helping the wrong person. And some of the latest research shows just how little effort certain attacks need.

The strange part is how normal it all looks until something goes wrong. A few attacks are clever. Others are almost embarrassingly simple. Both can do real damage. Here's what caught our attention this week.

Threat Actor Exploits VMware vCenter Flaw as a 0-Day — The suspected China-nexus cyber espionage group known as Armory Castle (formerly UNC5221) has been attributed to the zero-day exploitation of a critical security flaw in VMware vCenter Server in early September 2026. The vulnerability, tracked as CVE-2026-123890 (CVSS score: 9.8), is a case of authentication bypass in the VMware Directory Service that could be exploited to gain unauthorized access to the system. In October, Google described a campaign by Armory Castle to exploit organizations through BeyondTrust's Remote Support tool, using a zero-day to breach the edge appliance and steal secrets useful for network reconnaissance and downstream compromises within organizations. The latest findings show that BeyondTrust was merely an entry point to a deeper and more complex campaign that involved the deployment of backdoors on Linux machines.

Attackers now weaponize AI to launch personalized phishing attacks faster than employees can react. Fight AI with AI, using realistic simulations to train employees to spot and stop threats. Stop phishing before it becomes a breach.

Cursor Links Malicious Claude Extension Release to Fireworks AI Incident — Cursor has traced the origins of a security incident involving a malicious version of Anthropic's Claude Code extension in its marketplace to a breach at AI infrastructure provider Fireworks. "An attacker obtained an access key we had issued to Fireworks," Cursor said. "The key was exposed by Fireworks, and the attacker used it to copy data from a read-only storage bucket we used to share data with them." The Cursor incident is another indication of how the rapid adoption of AI-powered development tools and increasingly interconnected software ecosystems has worsened software supply chain risks.

Cold Relic Exploits Cloud Breaches to Target Organizations — The Russian cyberespionage group known as Cold Relic is turning cloud breaches into a springboard for targeting a victim organization's wider network of trusted relationships. In the first intrusion observed late last year, the attackers gained control of a cloud administrator account and harvested internal communications, employee details, and other organizational records. Cold Relic later used that information to impersonate an employee of the first organization to conduct a social engineering attack on an individual at a second organization. The campaigns "demonstrate the convergence of human-centric social engineering and cloud identity abuse," Google said. "By obtaining access to strategic personnel rosters and operational documents, Russian intelligence services turn legitimate business communications into attack vectors against trusted partners."

Malicious Web Pages Can Hijack Meta Muse — New research from the NebuSec research team has demonstrated that an attacker-controlled web page can be exploited to gain unauthorized access to user data associated with Meta Muse and compromise the underlying virtual machine (VM) in which it operates. "An attacker could retrieve conversation history and stored memories without triggering Muse's network approval prompts," NebuSec said . "The attack also allowed us to alter stored memories and conversation history, creating risks beyond unauthorized data access. By exploiting a vulnerability in the shared Linux kernel, we escaped the agent's runtime container and obtained root privileges in the underlying user VM." Meta has since addressed the issue.

New FastUnlock Windows Narrator LockScreen Bypass Detailed — Security researcher Abdelhamid Naceri (aka Chaotic Eclipse) published FastUnlock , a new unpatched Windows 11 zero-day that abuses the Narrator screen reader to get past the lock screen and execute code as the lock screen account "WsiAccount." The attack requires physical access and at least one account linked to an online Microsoft account.

GL.iNet GoodCloud Exploited to Serve Reverse Shells, Backdoors — Hackers have exploited a vulnerability in GL.iNet's GoodCloud cloud management platform to install malware, backdoors, and remote access tools on customers' routers and KVM devices."An unauthorized party exploited the vulnerability to access and install malicious software on some GL.iNet routers and KVM devices," GL.iNet said . "The attack path has been blocked. We fixed the cloud vulnerability and blocked the access used in this incident. Devices can no longer be newly infected through this attack path. The cloud-side fix alone does not remove malware from devices that were already infected." GL.iNet has not disclosed how the attackers exploited its cloud platform, how many customers were affected, or whether information was stolen as a result of these compromises. RemoteToHome said it examined 183 routers and found suspicious activity on 152, including 24 with root-level reverse shells, 47 with an older persistent backdoor, and 81 showing evidence that attacker-supplied commands had executed. It has also identified a potential command injection vulnerability in the GoodCloud client, although GL.iNet has not confirmed if this is the same vulnerability that was used in the attacks.

Bugs drop weekly, and the gap between a patch and an exploit is shrinking fast. These are the heavy hitters for the week: high-severity, widely used, or already being poked at in the wild.

Check the list, patch what you have, and hit the ones marked urgent first — CVE-2026-14978, CVE-2026-45110, CVE-2026-49401, CVE-2026-59890 (Unicode Normalization Collision on macOS), CVE-2026-114938 aka Java4Shell (Java 17, 21, 25, and 27), CVE-2026-28400 (Docker Model Runner), CVE-2026-123633, CVE-2026-123007 , CVE-2026-123634, CVE-2026-121810, CVE-2026-113425 aka NGINX2Root ( NGINX and NGINX Plus ), CVE-2026-120718 (F5 BIG-IP), CVE-2026-126822 , CVE-2026-126336 , CVE-2026-121812 , CVE-2026-123637 , CVE-2026-125272 (F5 BIG-IP APM), CVE-2026-120722 , CVE-2026-121805 , CVE-2026-120285 , CVE-2026-120292 , CVE-2026-116953 (F5 BIG-IP iRule), CVE-2026-125274 (F5 BIG-IP and BIG-IQ), CVE-2026-114819 (Oracle AI Database), CVE-2026-132825 aka Ruby2Shell (Ruby), CVE-2026-130963, CVE-2026-131031, CVE-2026-131060, CVE-2026-131008, CVE-2026-131054, CVE-2026-130975 (Google Chrome), CVE-2026-132165 (Drupal), CVE-2026-123890 , CVE-2026-119618 (Broadcom VMware), CVE-2026-114202, CVE-2026-114204, CVE-2026-114205 (ASUS Control Center Express Agent), CVE-2026-126574 (ASUS Control Center), CVE-2026-112115 (ASUS Armoury Crate), CVE-2026-99312 , CVE-2026-99362 (Linux kernel), CVE-2026-132798 (Temenos Application Framework Java), CVE-2026-134624 (N-able Mail Assure and SpamExperts Hosted Cloud), CVE-2026-131914 (Idunno.AntiCsrf), CVE-2026-125396 aka Upload2Shell (Ghostscript), CVE-2026-129975 ( GPGME ), GHSA-qc22-rmgc-vg94 (MONAI Label), CVE-2026-23871 (WhatsApp for Android and WhatsApp Business for Android), and CVE-2026-134814 (binary-parser).

6,000 Social Engineering Attacks Reveal Why MFA Still Fails to Stop Account Takeovers → AI-powered phishing and voice scams are evolving, while attackers exploit weaker authentication and recovery paths to bypass MFA. Join this webinar to uncover real attack techniques and learn how to prevent account takeovers.

8 Million Security Events Reveal Why Attackers Can Hide Behind Tools You Already Trust → Attackers abuse legitimate IT tools to evade detection, but only about 12 in a million uses are malicious. Join this webinar to learn how to distinguish real threats from normal activity without drowning in alerts.

Your GitHub Actions Are SHA-Pinned. But Are They Actually Secure? → SHA pinning locks code to a commit, but doesn't verify its author or origin. Attackers can exploit this trust gap to sneak malicious code past security checks. Join this webinar to learn how impostor commits threaten CI/CD pipelines and how to defend against them.

Google Cloud-owned VirusTotal has introduced new cipher-agnostic file-encryption verification across Windows and macOS sandboxes to check whether a suspicious file actually encrypts user docume

### AI Agents Are Creating a Shared Responsibility Gap
- Source: BleepingComputer | Published: 2026-11-23T15:00:10Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: ai_threat
- Summary: AI agents operate across multiple platforms, creating security gaps when no single vendor can see or control their actions. Token Security examines the shared responsibility problem and explains how organizations can better manage agent identities, permissions, and accountability. [...]
- Technical details: AI agents operate across multiple platforms, creating security gaps when no single vendor can see or control their actions. Token Security examines the shared responsibility problem and explains how organizations can better manage agent identities, permissions, and accountability.

Frontier AI labs are very clear about their responsibilities: making the model more robust, better guarded, and harder to hijack. They are also clear about what they can’t do, which is understand the business context of an action. An agent deleting files from Google Drive could be malicious or perfectly normal. They can't tell.

That raises questions about the shared responsibility model for Agentic AI. In cloud security, there is a clear separation of responsibilities between cloud vendors and their customers. If you copy data from AWS into SFDC, each vendor is responsible for the data's infrastructure security while they have it, but the customer is responsible for the action.

The AWS Shared Responsibility Model is a classic example.

The separation isn’t nearly as clean for AI agents. Agents act as intermediaries between you and the request, and that intermediary isn't deterministic by nature. It sends authenticated, authorized requests that can be compromised or rogue.

The first scenario is somewhat clear and goes back to what the AI labs try to prevent: a rogue agent has either misunderstood something or acted in a way it shouldn’t. A human mistake, only not human and not necessarily a mistake.

Five organizations have published guidance on responsibility for AI agents: shared responsibility models from Microsoft, CSA and CoSAI, AWS's security guidance for AgentCore, and Anthropic's “Trustworthy agents in practice.” Only two assign ownership of an agent’s identity, and they split it differently. No one agrees on who is responsible for the agent's behavior.

The available guidance approaches responsibility from different angles. Some publications assign specific tasks to providers and customers; others describe security layers or broader stakeholder roles.

Together, they show why organizations need to determine who owns each agent, what permissions it has, and how it operates across the systems it uses.

Consider a coding agent that reads a support ticket through an MCP server. That ticket contains malicious instructions that cause the agent to push a change to GitHub using the developer's token.

It isn't a contrived case. In the organizations Token monitors, 60% of Claude Code and Desktop agents called two or more outside vendors, and 47% called three or more, in the span of one month.

Now to the million-dollar question: who's responsible for catching the malicious push and stopping it?

Learn how security teams at Roblox, Amex, and more give each agent an owner, narrow its access, and cut it off fast, then see how Token Security does it in a live demo.

The software that runs the agent has the most visibility: it knows where the instructions were (albeit not why they were there) and the command about to run.

Claude Code, for example, checks both. A server-side probe "scans incoming tool results and flags suspicious content before Claude reads it." In auto mode, now the starting mode for interactive sessions, a separate classifier blocks actions that appear "driven by hostile content Claude read."

OpenAI, Cursor, and GitHub each have their own versions of approval prompts, automated reviewers, network limits, and sandboxes.

This capability doesn't come with responsibility, though. Harnesses put that squarely on the user's shoulders, both within the interface and in their terms.

The poisoned ticket came in over MCP, a protocol built for model clients. You might expect it to carry some of the load, because it's a door purpose-made for agents. At least conceptually, it could denote expanded responsibility for the people who built this door.

The MCP spec hands the load back to the client. Clients SHOULD validate tool results before passing them to the model, and "there SHOULD always be a human in the loop with the ability to deny tool invocations," it says.
- Fix / remediation: No specific fix identified in retrieved source text
- EPSS context: EPSS unavailable / not applicable — FIRST.org; not an asset-specific risk score

The xdg-desktop-portal packages provide a portal front-end service to flatpak. Thesis service exposes a series of D-Bus interfaces known as portals under a well-known name (org.freedesktop.portal.Desktop) and object path (/org/freedesktop/portal/desktop). The portal interfaces include APIs for file access, opening URIs, printing and others.

### Microsoft testing fix for Windows 11 Bluetooth audio quality drops
- Source: BleepingComputer | Published: 2026-11-23T13:28:45Z | Detail enrichment: enriched
- CVEs: none extracted | Topic signals: analyst review required
- Summary: Microsoft is working to fix a long-standing Windows issue that has been causing significant audio quality drops for Bluetooth headphone users. [...]
- Technical details: Microsoft is working to fix a long-standing Windows issue that has been causing significant audio quality drops for Bluetooth headphone users.

This is triggered when Windows automatically switches from the headphone (A2DP stereo) profile to the headset (hands-free, phone-call-like) profile when an app or game activates and uses the headset's microphone, because bandwidth is limited in two-way mode.

After installing the preview builds released for the Beta and Experimental channels on Friday, Windows 11 Insiders with newer Bluetooth headsets can keep playing high-quality audio even after the OS switches between profiles.

"Bluetooth LE Audio devices can now play at full-range stereo fidelity while also using the device's microphone, ensuring you continue to hear great audio quality when in game chat, in a voice call, using voice typing, or using voice chat in Copilot Chat," said Windows Insider communications lead Stephen Lines.

"When using an LE Audio device with a PC that supports Super Wideband Stereo, choose the new 2 channels, 16 bit, 48000Hz option from Settings > System > Sound > Output > {Bluetooth device} > Output settings > Format when microphone is active."

This new feature is available for all Bluetooth LE Audio devices that support 48kHz playback, such as headphones, earbuds, and speakers.

​Earlier this month, Microsoft also fixed USB audio issues that have plagued Windows users who installed security updates released since September.

As it explained when it acknowledged this known issue days after the September 2026 Patch Tuesday, USB Audio Class 1.0 devices failed to start or produce audio on some systems after installing recent updates.

Two years ago, Microsoft also blocked Windows 24H2 upgrades on systems with incompatible Intel Smart Sound Technology (SST) audio drivers due to blue screen of death (BSOD) issues and confirmed an issue that was causing game audio to go full volume when using USB DAC sound systems.

In January 2025, Microsoft addressed a bug causing audio playback problems and Code 10 errors on Windows 11 24H2 systems with USB audio device drivers after installing the KB5050094 preview update.

More recently, on Tuesday, Microsoft announced that it's testing a new feature that lets IT admins remotely lock stolen or lost enterprise Windows devices without wiping them.

Microsoft pushes Windows emergency updates to fix activation failures
